  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Orca di Ebuda adalah monster laut dalam Orlando furioso karya Ludovico Ariosto, mirip monster dalam mitos Perseus dan Andromeda. Ia tinggal di dasar laut dekat pulau Ebuda, dan penduduknya yang bajak laut mempersembahkan perempuan tawanan sebagai makanannya, sesuai perintah Proteus sejak dahulu kala. Wujudnya lebih mirip ular laut berkulit keras daripada paus orca.",
      "en": "The Orc of Ebuda is a sea monster in Ludovico Ariosto's Orlando furioso, like the monster in the myth of Perseus and Andromeda. It lives on the seabed by the isle of Ebuda, whose pirate inhabitants feed it captive women, as Proteus had commanded since ancient times. Its shape is more like a hard-skinned sea serpent than a killer whale.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "orc-of-ebuda-c02",
        "orc-of-ebuda-c03",
        "orc-of-ebuda-c06"

    },
    {
      "id": "Ruggero yang menunggang Hippogriff menyelamatkan Angelica dengan membuat monster itu pusing dengan perisainya. Orlando kemudian masuk ke mulutnya, mengganjal rahangnya dengan jangkar, dan membunuhnya dari dalam. Kisah ini mengilhami tragedi Gabriello Chiabrera dan lukisan Ingres.",
      "en": "Ruggero, riding the Hippogriff, saves Angelica by stunning the monster with his shield. Orlando later enters its mouth, wedges its jaws with an anchor and kills it from within. The episode inspired a tragedy by Gabriello Chiabrera and paintings by Ingres.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "orc-of-ebuda-c01",
      "source_id": "orc-of-ebuda-s1",
      "quote": "L'Orca di Ebuda è un mostro marino che appare nell'Orlando furioso di Ludovico Ariosto. Non si tratta di un esemplare di orca marina, ma di una vera e propria creatura mostruosa, molto simile a quella che appare nel mito concernente Perseo e Andromeda.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orca di Ebuda adalah monster laut dalam Orlando furioso karya Ludovico Ariosto; bukan paus orca, melainkan makhluk mengerikan yang sangat mirip monster dalam mitos Perseus dan Andromeda.",
        "en": "The Orc of Ebuda is a sea monster in Ludovico Ariosto's Orlando furioso; not a killer whale but a true monster, much like the one in the myth of Perseus and Andromeda."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "orc-of-ebuda-c02",
      "source_id": "orc-of-ebuda-s1",
      "quote": "l'Orca ha la sua dimora in fondo al mare, presso uno scoglio dell'isola di Ebuda (da identificare probabilmente con una delle Ebridi), i cui abitanti, dediti alla pirateria, offrono in pasto al mostro tutte le giovani donne da loro catturate.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orca tinggal di dasar laut dekat sebuah karang di pulau Ebuda (mungkin salah satu Hebrides), yang penduduknya bajak laut dan mempersembahkan semua perempuan muda tawanan mereka sebagai makanannya.",
        "en": "The Orc lives on the seabed by a rock of the isle of Ebuda (probably one of the Hebrides), whose piratical inhabitants feed it every young woman they capture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "orc-of-ebuda-c03",
      "source_id": "orc-of-ebuda-s1",
      "quote": "Il poeta inoltre precisa che tali sacrifici umani si protraevano da tempi antichissimi su imposizione di Proteo, a seguito dell'uccisione da parte di un re della sua stessa figlia, ingravidata dal dio marino.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengorbanan manusia ini sudah berlangsung sejak dahulu atas perintah Proteus, setelah seorang raja membunuh putrinya sendiri yang dihamili dewa laut itu.",
        "en": "These human sacrifices had gone on since ancient times at Proteus's command, after a king killed his own daughter, made pregnant by the sea god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "orc-of-ebuda-c04",
      "source_id": "orc-of-ebuda-s1",
      "quote": "la principessa del Catai trova la salvezza grazie a Ruggero, che in groppa all'Ippogrifo stordisce l'Orca con il suo scudo abbagliante per poi rompere le catene che tenevano Angelica avvinta allo scoglio.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Angelica, putri Cathay, diselamatkan Ruggero yang menunggang Hippogriff dan membuat Orca pusing dengan perisainya yang menyilaukan, lalu memutus rantai Angelica.",
        "en": "Angelica, princess of Cathay, is saved by Ruggero, who rides the Hippogriff, stuns the Orc with his dazzling shield and breaks her chains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "orc-of-ebuda-c05",
      "source_id": "orc-of-ebuda-s1",
      "quote": "dopo essere entrato nella sua bocca e averne bloccato le fauci con un'ancora,  il paladino riuscirà a uccidere l'Orca assestandole un colpo di spada dall'interno.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orlando masuk ke mulut monster itu, mengganjal rahangnya dengan jangkar, dan membunuhnya dengan tebasan pedang dari dalam.",
        "en": "Orlando enters its mouth, wedges its jaws open with an anchor and kills the Orc with a sword stroke from inside."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "orc-of-ebuda-c06",
      "source_id": "orc-of-ebuda-s2",
      "quote": "Forma bestiae potius similis formae serpentis marini quam formae orcinae est quia, ut ipse Ariostus scripsit, \"Orca formam animalem, praeter caput, non habet\" atque quia cutis monstri marini dura est.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wujudnya lebih mirip ular laut daripada orca, karena menurut Ariosto Orca tidak berbentuk hewan kecuali kepalanya, dan kulitnya keras.",
        "en": "Its shape is more like a sea serpent than an orca, because Ariosto wrote that the Orc has no animal form except its head, and its skin is hard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "orc-of-ebuda-c07",
      "source_id": "orc-of-ebuda-s1",
      "quote": "Il combattimento di Ruggero con l'Orca è presente anche nella tragedia Angelica in Ebuda del poeta barocco Gabriello Chiabrera e inoltre ha ispirato diversi pittori come Jean-Auguste-Dominique Ingres",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pertarungan Ruggero dengan Orca juga muncul dalam tragedi Angelica in Ebuda karya Gabriello Chiabrera dan mengilhami pelukis seperti Jean-Auguste-Dominique Ingres.",
        "en": "Ruggero's fight with the Orc also appears in Gabriello Chiabrera's tragedy Angelica in Ebuda and inspired painters such as Jean-Auguste-Dominique Ingres."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Petermännchen adalah roh rumah kecil yang baik hati penjaga Istana Schwerin, Jerman, yang membawa kunci, menghadiahi orang jujur, dan mengusir pencuri.",
    "en": "Petermännchen is the small, good-natured household spirit guarding Schwerin Palace in Germany, carrying keys, rewarding the honest and driving off thieves.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "petermannchen-c01",
      "petermannchen-c02",
      "petermannchen-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Petermännchen adalah roh rumah kecil yang baik hati di Istana Schwerin, Mecklenburg. Ia membawa banyak kunci dan berkeliaran di ruang bawah tanah serta terowongan istana. Sebagai penjaga istana ia menghadiahi orang jujur, menghukum pencuri dan penyusup, dan membangunkan prajurit yang tertidur saat jaga agar tidak dihukum.",
      "en": "Petermännchen is a small, good-natured household spirit of Schwerin Palace in Mecklenburg. He carries many keys and roams the castle's vaults and tunnels. As its watchman he rewards the honest, punishes thieves and intruders, and wakes sleeping sentries so they escape punishment.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "petermannchen-c01",
        "petermannchen-c02",
        "petermannchen-c03",
        "petermannchen-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda, ia satu-satunya roh pelayan dewa pagan yang tetap tinggal setelah agama Kristen datang. Richard Wossidlo menafsirkannya sebagai dewa Radegast yang menjadi kobold penjaga harta bangsa Obotrit. Ia konon mengganggu Wallenstein begitu hebat pada malam pertamanya di istana sehingga sang jenderal pergi esok harinya.",
      "en": "Legend makes him the only spirit servant of a pagan god who stayed after Christianity arrived. Richard Wossidlo read him as the god Radegast turned into a kobold guarding the Obotrites' treasure. He is said to have tormented Wallenstein so badly on his first night at the castle that the general left the next day.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "petermannchen-c05",
        "petermannchen-c06",
        "petermannchen-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Patung Petermännchen karya Heinrich Petters dari sekitar 1856 menghiasi fasad halaman istana.",
      "en": "A statue of Petermännchen by Heinrich Petters from around 1856 adorns the castle courtyard façade.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "petermannchen-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "petermannchen-c01",
      "source_id": "petermannchen-s1",
      "quote": "Petermännchen (English: \"Little Peterman\"; German: [ˈpeːtɐˌmɛnçən]) is a small good-natured household spirit who is said to live in the Schwerin Palace in Schwerin, Mecklenburg, Germany.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Petermännchen (\"Peterman kecil\") adalah roh rumah kecil yang baik hati yang konon tinggal di Istana Schwerin, Mecklenburg, Jerman.",
        "en": "Petermännchen (\"Little Peterman\") is a small, good-natured household spirit said to live in Schwerin Palace in Schwerin, Mecklenburg, Germany."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "petermannchen-c02",
      "source_id": "petermannchen-s1",
      "quote": "The diminutive spirit, maybe only a few feet tall, is often described as carrying many keys, roaming through the vaults and tunnels underneath the castle, unlocking doors as he goes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh mungil ini, mungkin hanya setinggi beberapa kaki, sering digambarkan membawa banyak kunci dan berkeliaran di ruang bawah tanah dan terowongan istana sambil membuka pintu-pintu.",
        "en": "The tiny spirit, perhaps only a few feet tall, is often described carrying many keys and roaming the vaults and tunnels under the castle, unlocking doors as he goes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "petermannchen-c03",
      "source_id": "petermannchen-s1",
      "quote": "He is also said to be the keeper and watchman of the castle, rewarding those who are honest and good. Conversely the spirit will punish or drive away thieves and intruders.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga penjaga istana yang memberi hadiah kepada orang jujur dan baik, sebaliknya menghukum atau mengusir pencuri dan penyusup.",
        "en": "He is also the keeper and watchman of the castle, rewarding the honest and good while punishing or driving away thieves and intruders."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "petermannchen-c04",
      "source_id": "petermannchen-s1",
      "quote": "Petermännchen is also said to have woken soldiers who fell asleep on their night-watch in order to save them from being punished.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Petermännchen konon membangunkan prajurit yang tertidur saat jaga malam agar mereka tidak dihukum.",
        "en": "Petermännchen is also said to have woken soldiers who fell asleep on night watch to save them from punishment."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "petermannchen-c05",
      "source_id": "petermannchen-s1",
      "quote": "Legend further has it that when Christianity first came to the Schwerin area, a pagan god, who was worshiped at the site of the present Schwerin castle, fled leaving his servants, the spirits. Eventually these spirits also departed, except for one: Petermännchen, who would not leave his post.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, saat agama Kristen tiba di Schwerin, dewa pagan yang dipuja di lokasi istana melarikan diri dan meninggalkan para roh pelayannya; semuanya akhirnya pergi kecuali Petermännchen yang tak mau meninggalkan posnya.",
        "en": "Legend says that when Christianity reached Schwerin, a pagan god worshipped where the castle now stands fled, leaving his spirit servants; all of them eventually left except Petermännchen, who would not abandon his post."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "petermannchen-c06",
      "source_id": "petermannchen-s2",
      "quote": "Das Petermännchen soll unter anderem Wallenstein, den Kaiser Ferdinand II. während des Dreißigjährigen Krieges 1628 mit dem Herzogtum Mecklenburg belehnte, während seiner ersten Nacht im Schloss Schwerin so arg gepiesackt haben, dass der große General am nächsten Morgen vollkommen übernächtigt wieder abreiste",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Petermännchen konon begitu mengganggu Wallenstein, yang dianugerahi Kadipaten Mecklenburg oleh Kaisar Ferdinand II pada 1628, pada malam pertamanya di Istana Schwerin sehingga sang jenderal pergi esok paginya dalam keadaan tak tidur.",
        "en": "Petermännchen is said to have tormented Wallenstein, granted the Duchy of Mecklenburg by Emperor Ferdinand II in 1628, so badly on his first night at Schwerin Palace that the general left the next morning utterly sleepless."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "petermannchen-c07",
      "source_id": "petermannchen-s2",
      "quote": "Richard Wossidlo deutete einige Petermännchen-Sagen so, dass das Petermännchen der in einen Kobold verwandelte Gott Radegast ist, zurückgeblieben, um den heiligen Schatz der Obotriten und die Angehörigen des Volkes zu schützen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Richard Wossidlo menafsirkan beberapa legenda bahwa Petermännchen adalah dewa Radegast yang berubah menjadi kobold dan tinggal untuk menjaga harta suci bangsa Obotrit.",
        "en": "Richard Wossidlo interpreted some legends as making Petermännchen the god Radegast turned into a kobold, left behind to guard the sacred treasure of the Obotrites."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "petermannchen-c08",
      "source_id": "petermannchen-s2",
      "quote": "Eine von Heinrich Petters um 1856 geschaffene Petermännchen-Statue befindet sich in der Hoffassade des Schlosses.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patung Petermännchen karya Heinrich Petters dari sekitar 1856 terpasang di fasad halaman istana.",
        "en": "A statue of Petermännchen made by Heinrich Petters around 1856 stands on the castle's courtyard façade."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Piztia adalah binatang buas legendaris di hutan Domaikia, Álava, yang tinggal di gua gunung Carrascal dan memangsa anak-anak dan ternak pada malam hari.",
    "en": "The Piztia is a legendary wild beast of the woods of Domaikia in Álava that lives in a cave on Mount Carrascal and devours children and livestock at night.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "piztia-c01",
      "piztia-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Piztia atau La Pistia adalah binatang liar legendaris yang berkeliaran di hutan sekitar Domaikia, Zuia (Álava). Ia tinggal di gua di tebing Iruratxi, gunung Carrascal, dan membuat anak-anak serta ternak menghilang; siang hari ia tidak berbahaya, tetapi malam hari melahap apa pun. Ada dugaan legenda ini berasal dari beruang, atau versi lokal Tartalo atau Anxo.",
      "en": "The Piztia or La Pistia is a legendary wild beast roaming the woods around Domaikia in Zuia (Álava). It lives in a cave on the Iruratxi crag of Mount Carrascal and makes children and livestock vanish; harmless by day, at night it devours everything. Some think a bear lies behind the legend, or that it is a local Tartalo or Anxo.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "piztia-c01",
        "piztia-c02",
        "piztia-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada abad ke-18 warga Domaikia memohon kepada Bunda Maria dari Oro agar teror Pistia berakhir, dan dari situ lahir \"Lagu Pistia\" yang masih dinyanyikan.",
      "en": "In the 18th century the people of Domaikia prayed to Our Lady of Oro to end the Pistia's terror, giving rise to the \"Song of the Pistia\" still sung today.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "piztia-c04",
        "piztia-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "piztia-c01",
      "source_id": "piztia-s1",
      "quote": "La Piztia ('bèstia, fera, feristela' en basc), anomenada en castellà La Pistia, és una bèstia salvatge llegendària que vaga pels boscos dels voltants de Domaikia, a Zuia (Àlaba).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Piztia (\"binatang buas\" dalam bahasa Basque), dalam bahasa Spanyol La Pistia, adalah binatang liar legendaris yang berkeliaran di hutan sekitar Domaikia, Zuia (Álava).",
        "en": "The Piztia ('beast' in Basque), called La Pistia in Spanish, is a legendary wild beast that roams the woods around Domaikia in Zuia (Álava)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "piztia-c02",
      "source_id": "piztia-s1",
      "quote": "Es diu que viu en una cova a la penya Iruratxi, al mont Carrascal, i que fa desaparèixer nens i bestiar. No és perillosa de dia, però de nit es diu que devora tot el que troba.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia konon tinggal di gua di tebing Iruratxi, gunung Carrascal, dan membuat anak-anak serta ternak menghilang; siang hari ia tidak berbahaya, tetapi malam hari melahap apa pun yang ditemuinya.",
        "en": "It is said to live in a cave on the Iruratxi crag of Mount Carrascal and to make children and livestock vanish; harmless by day, by night it devours everything it finds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "piztia-c03",
      "source_id": "piztia-s1",
      "quote": "S'ha especulat que podria tractar-se d'un animal salvatge real (un ós, per exemple) que hauria donat lloc a la llegenda, però també que podria ser una variant local del mite del Tartalo o Anxo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada dugaan legenda ini berasal dari hewan liar nyata, misalnya beruang, atau bahwa ia versi lokal mitos Tartalo atau Anxo.",
        "en": "Some speculate a real wild animal, such as a bear, gave rise to the legend, or that it is a local variant of the Tartalo or Anxo myth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "piztia-c04",
      "source_id": "piztia-s1",
      "quote": "Quan li va tocar a Domaikia, les seves rogatives a la Mare de Déu es van centrar a demanar la fi del malson de la Pistia. És d'aquella ocasió que data el «Cant de la Pistia» que ha arribat als nostres dies.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada abad ke-18, ketika tiba giliran Domaikia memimpin misa di Suaka Bunda Maria dari Oro, doa mereka berpusat pada permohonan agar teror Pistia berakhir, dan dari peristiwa itulah lahir \"Lagu Pistia\" yang masih ada sampai sekarang.",
        "en": "In the 18th century, when Domaikia's turn came to lead the Mass at the Sanctuary of Our Lady of Oro, its prayers centred on ending the Pistia nightmare, and the surviving \"Song of the Pistia\" dates from that occasion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "piztia-c05",
      "source_id": "piztia-s2",
      "quote": "Soberana Virgen de Oro, líbranos de todo mal, te lo pedimos a coro, de la Pistia del Carrascal",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lagu itu dibuka dengan permohonan kepada Perawan dari Oro agar melindungi mereka dari Pistia di Carrascal.",
        "en": "The song opens by begging the Virgin of Oro to deliver them from the Pistia of Carrascal."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Plafalgàs adalah raksasa baik hati dalam mitologi Katalan dari Vallfogona de Ripollès yang memikul lonceng gereja berisi gandum ke desanya.",
    "en": "Plafalgàs is a kind-hearted giant of Catalan mythology from Vallfogona de Ripollès who carried a church bell full of grain to his village.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "plafalgas-c01",
      "plafalgas-c02",
      "plafalgas-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Plafalgàs atau Pare Falgars adalah raksasa mitologi Katalan yang terkenal karena kebaikan, keberanian, dan kekuatannya yang ia pakai untuk menolong tetangga. Versi paling umum menempatkannya sebagai penggarap rumah pertanian di Vallfogona de Ripollès.",
      "en": "Plafalgàs or Pare Falgars is a giant of Catalan mythology famed for his kindness, courage and strength, which he uses to help his neighbours. The most common version makes him a farm tenant in Vallfogona de Ripollès.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "plafalgas-c01",
        "plafalgas-c02",
        "plafalgas-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pastor paroki memintanya memikul lonceng dari pertapaan Milany, lonceng yang kelak dilebur menjadi empat lonceng. Di tengah jalan petani mengejeknya, lalu ia bertaruh dan membawa lonceng itu diisi tujuh quartera gandum sampai ke desa. Ia dimakamkan di depan pintu gereja Sant Julià di bawah batu berukir lonceng, dan sejak 2009 desa itu punya boneka raksasa pesta yang memikul lonceng untuk mengenangnya.",
      "en": "The rector asked him to carry the bell from the Milany hermitage, a bell later melted into four. Teased by farmers on the way, he made a bet and carried it, filled with seven quarteres of grain, all the way to the village. He is buried before the door of Sant Julià under a slab carved with a bell, and since 2009 the village has a festival giant carrying a bell in his memory.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "plafalgas-c03",
        "plafalgas-c04",
        "plafalgas-c05",
        "plafalgas-c06",
        "plafalgas-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {

    {

      "title": "Plafalgàs",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (sv)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "sv",

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "plafalgas-c01",
      "source_id": "plafalgas-s1",
      "quote": "Plafalgàs o Plafalgars, també dit Pare Falgars, és un gegant de la mitologia catalana famós per la seva bonhomia, la seva valentia i la seva força prodigiosa, que posa al servei dels seus veïns.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Plafalgàs atau Plafalgars, disebut juga Pare Falgars, adalah raksasa mitologi Katalan yang terkenal karena kebaikan, keberanian, dan kekuatan luar biasanya yang ia pakai untuk menolong tetangganya.",
        "en": "Plafalgàs or Plafalgars, also called Pare Falgars, is a giant of Catalan mythology famed for his kindness, courage and prodigious strength, which he puts at his neighbours' service."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "plafalgas-c02",
      "source_id": "plafalgas-s1",
      "quote": "La versió més estesa de la llegenda el situa a Vallfogona de Ripollès. Plafalgàs era el masover del mas del mateix nom, avui ja enderrocat, prop de l'ermita de Santa Magdalena de Cambrils.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi paling umum menempatkannya di Vallfogona de Ripollès, sebagai penggarap rumah pertanian bernama sama dekat pertapaan Santa Magdalena de Cambrils.",
        "en": "The most widespread version places him in Vallfogona de Ripollès, as the tenant of the farmhouse of the same name near the hermitage of Santa Magdalena de Cambrils."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "plafalgas-c03",
      "source_id": "plafalgas-s1",
      "quote": "El rector de Sant Julià, que li encarregava feines diverses, li va encomanar la tasca de transportar la campana. Plafalgàs se la va carregar a l'espatlla i va emprendre el camí de tornada al poble.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pastor paroki Sant Julià menugaskannya mengangkut lonceng dari pertapaan kastel Milany, dan Plafalgàs memikulnya di bahu pulang ke desa.",
        "en": "The rector of Sant Julià gave him the task of carrying the bell from the Milany castle hermitage, and Plafalgàs shouldered it and set off back to the village."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "plafalgas-c04",
      "source_id": "plafalgas-s1",
      "quote": "Per tenir idea de les dimensions de la campana, només cal saber que va ser fosa i en van sortir les quatre que, abans de l'any 1936, hi havia al campanar de la parròquia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lonceng itu begitu besar sehingga setelah dilebur menjadi empat lonceng yang tergantung di menara gereja sampai 1936.",
        "en": "The bell was so big that, when melted down, it made the four bells that hung in the parish tower until 1936."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "plafalgas-c05",
      "source_id": "plafalgas-s1",
      "quote": "Plafalgàs els va desafiar a que, si li omplien la campana amb set quarteres de gra i ho transportava tot fins a la vila, es quedaria el blat. Acceptada la juguesca, valent-se d'un buscall de faig va anar fins a Vallfogona amb la campana i el gra.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ditantang petani yang mengejeknya, Plafalgàs bertaruh bahwa bila lonceng itu diisi tujuh quartera gandum dan ia sanggup membawanya ke desa, gandum itu menjadi miliknya; dengan bantuan sebatang kayu beech ia membawa lonceng dan gandum itu ke Vallfogona.",
        "en": "Teased by farmers, Plafalgàs bet that if they filled the bell with seven quarteres of grain and he carried it all to the village, the wheat would be his; leaning on a beech staff he carried bell and grain to Vallfogona."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "plafalgas-c06",
      "source_id": "plafalgas-s1",
      "quote": "Segons la tradició, Plafalgàs està enterrat just al davant del llindar de la porta de l'església de Sant Julià —on solia seure en una enorme pila de pedra—, sota una llosa on hi ha gravada una campana.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut tradisi Plafalgàs dimakamkan tepat di depan ambang pintu gereja Sant Julià, di bawah batu nisan berukir lonceng.",
        "en": "Tradition says Plafalgàs is buried right before the threshold of the church of Sant Julià, under a slab carved with a bell."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "plafalgas-c07",
      "source_id": "plafalgas-s1",
      "quote": "L'any 2009, el poble va retre homenatge al seu personatge llegendari fent construir al mestre Tavi Algueró un gegant de festa major de més de tres metres d'alçada",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 2009 desa itu menghormati tokoh legendarisnya dengan memesan boneka raksasa pesta setinggi lebih dari tiga meter dari Tavi Algueró.",
        "en": "In 2009 the village honoured its legendary figure by having master Tavi Algueró build a festival giant over three metres tall."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "plafalgas-c08",
      "source_id": "plafalgas-s2",
      "quote": "Han är känd för sin godhet, sitt mod och sin enorma styrka, som han använder till att hjälpa sina grannar.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikenal karena kebaikan, keberanian, dan kekuatan besarnya yang ia pakai untuk menolong tetangga.",
        "en": "He is known for his goodness, courage and enormous strength, which he uses to help his neighbours."
      }
    }

Ia menguasai pintu masuk, terutama pelabuhan, sehingga digambarkan menggenggam kunci. Di pelabuhan Tiber ia punya kuil tempat perayaan Portumnalia diadakan setiap 17 Agustus.",
      "en": "Portunus or Portumno was a sea god worshipped on the coasts of Italy by Etruscans and Romans. He was identified with Palaemon or Melicertes and sometimes with Neptune.       "source_id": "portumno-s1",
      "quote": "Portumno o Portuno era el nombre de un dios marino adorado en las costas de Italia, principalmente entre los etruscos y romanos.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Portumno atau Portunus adalah dewa laut yang dipuja di pesisir Italia, terutama oleh orang Etruria dan Romawi.",
        "en": "Portumno or Portunus was a sea god worshipped on the coasts of Italy, chiefly among the Etruscans and Romans."

      "source_id": "portumno-s1",

      "context": "religious-tradition",

      }
    },

      "source_id": "portumno-s1",

      "statement": {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Rahovart adalah iblis pendamping Setan dalam Dictionnaire Infernal yang menyiksa orang kikir dan menyimpan jiwa korbannya dalam keranjang.",
    "en": "Rahovart is a demon companion of Satan in the Dictionnaire Infernal who torments misers and keeps his victims' souls in a basket.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "rahovart-c01",
      "rahovart-c02",
      "rahovart-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Rahovart atau Rahouart adalah iblis pendamping Setan yang disebut dalam Dictionnaire Infernal karya Collin de Plancy (1818), dengan rujukan ke Moralité karya Durzel, buku Renaisans yang dicetak di Rouen. Ia menyiksa orang jahat, orang kaya yang kikir, dan orang tua pemarah, serta bertanggung jawab atas longsoran. Orang tua paling mudah dirasukinya, dan jiwa mereka yang mati di bawah pengaruhnya disimpan dalam keranjangnya sampai hari kiamat.",
      "en": "Rahovart or Rahouart is a demon companion of Satan named in Collin de Plancy's Dictionnaire Infernal (1818), citing Durzel's Moralité, a Renaissance book printed in Rouen. He torments the wicked, rich misers and old curmudgeons, and is charged with avalanches. The elderly are most easily possessed by him, and the souls of those who die under his influence are kept in his basket until Judgement Day.",

        "rahovart-c02",
        "rahovart-c03",
        "rahovart-c04"
      ]

      "id": "rahovart-c01",
      "source_id": "rahovart-s1",
      "quote": "Rahovart (or Rahouart) is a demon identified in Collin de Plancy's Dictionnaire Infernal (1818), according to which he is referenced in Durzel's Moralité, a Renaissance book. Rahovart is a companion of Satan; little is known about the demon himself.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rahovart (atau Rahouart) adalah iblis yang disebut dalam Dictionnaire Infernal karya Collin de Plancy (1818), yang merujuk Moralité karya Durzel dari masa Renaisans; ia pendamping Setan dan hanya sedikit yang diketahui tentangnya.",
        "en": "Rahovart (or Rahouart) is a demon named in Collin de Plancy's Dictionnaire Infernal (1818), which cites Durzel's Renaissance book Moralité; he is a companion of Satan and little is known about him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahovart-c02",
      "source_id": "rahovart-s1",
      "quote": "Rahovart is attributed to the torment of evil, rich misers and old curmudgeons. He is also charged with avalanches and ghostly torment.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rahovart bertugas menyiksa orang jahat, orang kaya yang kikir, dan orang tua pemarah, juga bertanggung jawab atas longsoran dan siksaan gaib.",
        "en": "Rahovart is assigned to torment the wicked, rich misers and old curmudgeons, and is also charged with avalanches and ghostly torment."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahovart-c03",
      "source_id": "rahovart-s1",
      "quote": "Reportedly, the elderly are most easily subject to possession by this demon, and if the person should die under the demonic effect, their souls are collected into Rahovart's basket until the day of judgment.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang tua konon paling mudah dirasuki iblis ini, dan bila mereka mati di bawah pengaruhnya, jiwa mereka dikumpulkan dalam keranjang Rahovart sampai hari kiamat.",
        "en": "The elderly are reportedly most easily possessed by this demon, and if they die under its influence their souls go into Rahovart's basket until Judgement Day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahovart-c04",
      "source_id": "rahovart-s2",
      "quote": "Rahovart, ou \"Senhor Rahovart\", é um demônio e companheiro de Satanás. Ele é referenciado no Moralidade de Durzel, um livro do Renascimento impresso em Rouen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rahovart atau \"Tuan Rahovart\" adalah iblis pendamping Setan yang disebut dalam Moralité karya Durzel, buku Renaisans yang dicetak di Rouen.",
        "en": "Rahovart, or \"Lord Rahovart\", is a demon and companion of Satan mentioned in Durzel's Moralité, a Renaissance book printed in Rouen."
      }
    }

    "en": "Ryangombe is a demigod and warrior chief of the tradition of Rwanda who after death led the Imandwa spirits, intermediaries between Imana and humans.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]

  "long_description": [

      "id": "Ryangombe adalah setengah dewa, pejuang, dan pemimpin yang dikagumi semasa hidupnya. Setelah wafat ia menjadi pemimpin Imandwa, kelompok roh perantara antara Imana dan manusia. Ia datang ke Rwanda dari Murilo Kitara di Uganda barat dan pertama kali muncul pada masa raja Ruganzu II Ndoli sekitar 1510 untuk menyebarkan agamanya.",
      "en": "Ryangombe is a demigod, warrior and chief admired in life. After death he became leader of the Imandwa, spirits mediating between Imana and humans. He came to Rwanda from Murilo Kitara in western Uganda, first appearing under King Ruganzu II Ndoli around 1510 to spread his religion.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ryangombe-c01",
        "ryangombe-c02",
        "ryangombe-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ayahnya Babinga wa Nyundo, ibunya Nyiraryangombe, dan istrinya Nyirakajumba; ia punya lima anak, antara lain Binego. Ia dikenal sebagai asal-usul ritual kubandwa dan guterekera.",
      "en": "His father was Babinga wa Nyundo, his mother Nyiraryangombe and his wife Nyirakajumba; he had five children, including Binego. He is known as the origin of the kubandwa and guterekera rites.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "ryangombe-c06"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ryangombe-c01",
      "source_id": "ryangombe-s1",
      "quote": "Ryangombe est un demi-dieu, guerrier et chef, admiré de son vivant, qui, après sa mort, devint le chef des Imandwa, un groupe d’esprits qui servent d’intermédiaires entre Imana et les humains.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ryangombe adalah setengah dewa, pejuang, dan pemimpin yang dikagumi semasa hidupnya, yang setelah wafat menjadi pemimpin Imandwa, kelompok roh perantara antara Imana dan manusia.",
        "en": "Ryangombe is a demigod, warrior and chief admired in life, who after death became leader of the Imandwa, a group of spirits mediating between Imana and humans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ryangombe-c02",
      "source_id": "ryangombe-s1",
      "quote": "Il est venu au Rwanda depuis l'ouest de l'Ouganda, dans le district de Murilo Kitara .",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia datang ke Rwanda dari Uganda barat, dari distrik Murilo Kitara.",
        "en": "He came to Rwanda from western Uganda, from the district of Murilo Kitara."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ryangombe-c03",
      "source_id": "ryangombe-s1",
      "quote": "Il est apparu pour la première fois au Rwanda sous le règne du roi Ruganzu II Ndoli vers 1510, lorsqu'il est venu au Rwanda pour diffuser sa religion.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia pertama kali muncul di Rwanda pada masa raja Ruganzu II Ndoli sekitar 1510, ketika datang untuk menyebarkan agamanya.",
        "en": "He first appeared in Rwanda under King Ruganzu II Ndoli around 1510, when he came to spread his religion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ryangombe-c04",
      "source_id": "ryangombe-s1",
      "quote": "Le père de Ryangombe s'appelait Babinga wa Nyundo, sa mère s'appelait Nyiraryangombe wa Umusumbakazi et sa femme s'appelait Nyirakajumba.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ayah Ryangombe bernama Babinga wa Nyundo, ibunya Nyiraryangombe wa Umusumbakazi, dan istrinya Nyirakajumba.",
        "en": "Ryangombe's father was Babinga wa Nyundo, his mother Nyiraryangombe wa Umusumbakazi and his wife Nyirakajumba."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ryangombe-c05",
      "source_id": "ryangombe-s1",
      "quote": "Ryangombe est un enfant unique. Il eut lui-même cinq enfants : Binego, Ruhanga, Nyabirungu, Mashira et Kagoro.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ryangombe anak tunggal dan punya lima anak: Binego, Ruhanga, Nyabirungu, Mashira, dan Kagoro.",
        "en": "Ryangombe was an only child and had five children: Binego, Ruhanga, Nyabirungu, Mashira and Kagoro."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ryangombe-c06",
      "source_id": "ryangombe-s2",
      "quote": "Icyo azwiho cyane ni uko yabaye inkomoko yo kubandwa no guterekera kuko mbere yo gupfa kwe bitabagaho",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Kinyarwanda, ia paling dikenal sebagai asal-usul ritual kubandwa dan guterekera, yang belum ada sebelum ia wafat.",
        "en": "According to the Kinyarwanda article, he is best known as the origin of the kubandwa and guterekera rites, which did not exist before his death."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tac adalah makhluk usil pengubah wujud dari Landes, Gascogne, yang menjadi kuda untuk menenggelamkan penunggangnya.",
    "en": "The tac is a mischievous shapeshifter of the Landes in Gascony that turns into a horse to drown its riders.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tac-c01",
      "tac-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tac adalah makhluk khayalan legenda Gascogne, terutama Landes, yang berkerabat dengan drac Occitania dan mirip lébérou serta ganipote. Ia usil, menipu manusia, dan menertawakan kepolosan mereka. Ia dapat menjadi keranjang, anak domba, atau anak kambing, dan kadang kuda yang terjun ke sungai untuk menenggelamkan penunggangnya; hanya wujud jembatan yang terlarang baginya.",
      "en": "The tac is a fantastic being of Gascon legend, especially in the Landes, related to the drac of Occitania and close to the lébérou and the ganipote. It is mischievous, tricking humans and laughing at their gullibility. It can become a basket, a lamb or a kid, and sometimes a horse that leaps into a river to drown its riders; only the form of a bridge is forbidden to it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tac-c01",
        "tac-c02",
        "tac-c03",
        "tac-c04",
        "tac-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia muncul pada malam hari di hutan atau dekat mata air, bersiul meniru alat penyadap getah, dan berusaha digendong orang yang lewat sampai korbannya kelelahan atau mati.",
      "en": "It appears at night in woods or near springs, whistling like a resin tapper's tool, and tries to ride on passers-by until the victim is exhausted or dies.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tac-c05",
        "tac-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

      "published": null,
      "language": "ca",

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tac-c01",
      "source_id": "tac-s1",
      "quote": "Le tac (en gascon lo tac) est un être fantastique appartenant aux légendes et contes de la Gascogne et particulièrement du département des Landes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tac (bahasa Gascon: lo tac) adalah makhluk khayalan dalam legenda dan dongeng Gascogne, terutama departemen Landes.",
        "en": "The tac (Gascon lo tac) is a fantastic being of the legends and tales of Gascony, especially the Landes department."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tac-c02",
      "source_id": "tac-s1",
      "quote": "L'origine du mot reste inconnue. Il est apparenté au drac qu'on retrouve dans toute l'Occitanie. Il se rapproche du lébérou (Périgord) et de la ganipote (Gironde, Charentes, Poitou).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal katanya tidak diketahui; ia berkerabat dengan drac yang dikenal di seluruh Occitania, dan mirip lébérou (Périgord) dan ganipote (Gironde, Charentes, Poitou).",
        "en": "The word's origin is unknown; it is related to the drac found throughout Occitania and close to the lébérou (Périgord) and the ganipote (Gironde, Charentes, Poitou)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tac-c03",
      "source_id": "tac-s1",
      "quote": "Il est capable de prendre différentes formes : panier, agneau, chevreau. Il peut également prendre parfois la forme d'un cheval, entraînant ainsi, en se jetant dans une rivière, la noyade de ceux qui ont le malheur de le monter.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dapat berubah wujud menjadi keranjang, anak domba, atau anak kambing, dan kadang menjadi kuda yang terjun ke sungai sehingga penunggangnya tenggelam.",
        "en": "It can take various forms, a basket, a lamb, a kid, and sometimes a horse that throws itself into a river, drowning those unlucky enough to ride it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tac-c04",
      "source_id": "tac-s1",
      "quote": "La seule forme qui lui est interdite est celle d'un pont car il entraînerait la mort d'un nombre trop important de victimes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Satu-satunya wujud yang terlarang baginya adalah jembatan, karena akan menewaskan terlalu banyak korban.",
        "en": "The only form forbidden to it is a bridge, because that would kill too many victims."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tac-c05",
      "source_id": "tac-s1",
      "quote": "Il se manifeste la nuit dans les bois ou près des fontaines des Landes. Il peut alors siffler quand quelqu'un vient en imitant le bruit du hapchot, outil du gemmeur landais.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul pada malam hari di hutan atau dekat mata air Landes dan bersiul meniru bunyi hapchot, alat penyadap getah pinus Landes.",
        "en": "It appears at night in the woods or near the springs of the Landes, whistling like the hapchot, the tool of the Landes resin tapper."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tac-c06",
      "source_id": "tac-s1",
      "quote": "Il cherche toujours  à se faire porter sur le dos de la personne qui passe et ceci jusqu'à l'épuisement et parfois le trépas de sa victime.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia selalu berusaha digendong di punggung orang yang lewat sampai korbannya kelelahan dan kadang mati.",
        "en": "It always tries to get carried on a passer-by's back until the victim is exhausted and sometimes dies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tac-c07",
      "source_id": "tac-s2",
      "quote": "És una mena d'ésser entremaliat que enganya els humans i se'n riu de la seva credulitat",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia makhluk usil yang menipu manusia dan menertawakan kepolosan mereka.",
        "en": "It is a mischievous being that tricks humans and laughs at their gullibility."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tinilau atau Tinirau adalah dewa Polinesia penjaga ikan yang kisah cintanya dengan Sina dikenal dari Samoa sampai Selandia Baru.",
    "en": "Tinilau or Tinirau is the Polynesian guardian of fish, whose love story with Sina is told from Samoa to New Zealand.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tinilau-c01",
      "tinilau-c08"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tinilau atau Tinirau adalah dewa Polinesia penjaga ikan yang dikenal di Samoa, Hawaii, Selandia Baru, dan Kepulauan Cook, dengan nama lain seperti Tigilau dan Sinilau. Kisahnya sering berkisar pada pencarian istri ke negeri lain atau penolakan; istrinyalah yang memberi tiap ikan ciri khasnya. Dalam sebagian tradisi ia berwatak ganda sebagai penjaga hiu yang merusak dan pemuda tampan, atau setengah manusia setengah ikan.",
      "en": "Tinilau or Tinirau is the Polynesian guardian of fish, known in Samoa, Hawaii, New Zealand and the Cook Islands, with other names such as Tigilau and Sinilau. His stories often turn on journeys to seek a spouse or on rejection; it is his wife who gives each fish its traits. In some traditions he is dual, a destructive guardian of sharks and a handsome youth, or half human and half fish.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tinilau-c01",
        "tinilau-c02",
        "tinilau-c03",
        "tinilau-c04",
        "tinilau-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah Samoa, ‘Ae dari Tonga membunuh dua penyu pinjaman Tinilau, lalu dewa-dewa pembalas dari Savaii membawanya kembali ke rumah Tinilau. Dalam kisah lain, Tigilau pergi ke matahari untuk merebut kembali jiwa istrinya Sina yang dicuri istrinya yang cemburu.",
      "en": "In a Samoan tale ‘Ae of Tonga kills Tinilau's two borrowed turtles, and the avenging gods of Savaii bring him back to Tinilau's house. In another, Tigilau travels to the sun to recover the soul of his wife Sina, stolen by a jealous co-wife.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tinilau-c05",
        "tinilau-c06",
        "tinilau-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "tinilau-s1",

    {
      "id": "tinilau-s2",
      "url": "https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tinilau",
      "title": "Tinilau",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (fr)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "fr",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tinilau-c01",
      "source_id": "tinilau-s1",
      "quote": "In Polynesian mythology, stories about Tinirau are found throughout the islands of Polynesia. He is a guardian of fish.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Polinesia, kisah tentang Tinirau ditemukan di seluruh kepulauan Polinesia; ia penjaga ikan.",
        "en": "In Polynesian mythology, stories of Tinirau are found throughout the islands of Polynesia; he is a guardian of fish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tinilau-c02",
      "source_id": "tinilau-s1",
      "quote": "Often he travels to another land in search of his wife, or his wife travels to another land in search of him; sometimes he treats his wife badly, or she rejects him; while he is guardian of fish, it is his wife who gives the fish their individual characteristics.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sering ia pergi ke negeri lain mencari istrinya, atau istrinya mencarinya; kadang ia memperlakukan istrinya dengan buruk atau ditolak; ia penjaga ikan, tetapi istrinyalah yang memberi tiap ikan ciri khasnya.",
        "en": "Often he travels abroad to seek his wife or she seeks him; sometimes he mistreats her or she rejects him; he guards the fish, but it is his wife who gives each fish its characteristics."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tinilau-c03",
      "source_id": "tinilau-s1",
      "quote": "In some traditions, he has a dual nature; one destructive as the guardian of sharks, and one a kind, handsome male youth; in others, his right side is human and his left side is a fish.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian tradisi ia berwatak ganda, sisi perusak sebagai penjaga hiu dan sisi pemuda tampan yang baik; dalam tradisi lain sisi kanannya manusia dan sisi kirinya ikan.",
        "en": "In some traditions he has a dual nature, destructive as guardian of sharks and a kind, handsome youth; in others his right side is human and his left a fish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tinilau-c04",
      "source_id": "tinilau-s1",
      "quote": "Alternate names in the various Polynesian languages include Kinilau, Sinilau, Tigilau, and Tinilau.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama lainnya dalam berbagai bahasa Polinesia antara lain Kinilau, Sinilau, Tigilau, dan Tinilau.",
        "en": "Other names in various Polynesian languages include Kinilau, Sinilau, Tigilau and Tinilau."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tinilau-c05",
      "source_id": "tinilau-s1",
      "quote": "Tinilau lends his two turtles to ‘Ae, a Tongan. When he gets home, ‘Ae kills the turtles and has a great feast for his people.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah Samoa, Tinilau meminjamkan dua penyunya kepada ‘Ae dari Tonga, yang membunuh penyu-penyu itu untuk pesta bagi rakyatnya.",
        "en": "In a Samoan tale Tinilau lends his two turtles to ‘Ae of Tonga, who kills them for a great feast for his people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tinilau-c06",
      "source_id": "tinilau-s1",
      "quote": "When a bloody wave washes up on the beach, Tinilau calls a meeting of all the avenging gods of Savaii. The gods go to Tonga and pick 'Ae up, and return him to the house of Tinilau.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika ombak berdarah sampai ke pantai, Tinilau mengumpulkan dewa-dewa pembalas dari Savaii yang lalu menculik ‘Ae dari Tonga dan membawanya ke rumah Tinilau.",
        "en": "When a bloody wave washes ashore, Tinilau summons the avenging gods of Savaii, who seize ‘Ae from Tonga and bring him to Tinilau's house."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tinilau-c07",
      "source_id": "tinilau-s1",
      "quote": "One day, Tigilau goes out fishing, and one of the jealous wives steals Sina's soul. To get it back, Tigilau travels to the sun",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suatu hari saat Tigilau memancing, salah satu istrinya yang cemburu mencuri jiwa Sina, dan Tigilau pergi ke matahari untuk mengambilnya kembali.",
        "en": "One day while Tigilau is fishing, a jealous wife steals Sina's soul, and Tigilau travels to the sun to get it back."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tinilau-c08",
      "source_id": "tinilau-s2",
      "quote": "Tinilau ou Tinirau est un dieu polynésien gardien des poissons, dans la culture de l'île de Samoa, de Hawaï ou dans la mythologie maorie de Nouvelle-Zélande et des Îles Cook.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tinilau atau Tinirau adalah dewa Polinesia penjaga ikan dalam budaya Samoa, Hawaii, dan mitologi Maori Selandia Baru serta Kepulauan Cook.",
        "en": "Tinilau or Tinirau is a Polynesian god, guardian of fish, in the culture of Samoa and Hawaii and in the Māori mythology of New Zealand and the Cook Islands."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en dan fr; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}
```

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tomiyauhtecuhtli adalah dewa Mexica rumput-rumputan, salah satu dari empat Tlaloque pembantu Tlaloc yang membagikan hujan.",
    "en": "Tomiyauhtecuhtli is the Mexica god of grasses, one of the four Tlaloque who help Tlaloc distribute the rain.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c01",
      "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tomiyauhtecuhtli, \"tuan bulir-bulir kami\", adalah dewa Mexica rumput-rumputan dan teki-tekian, salah satu dari empat Tlaloque pembantu Tlaloc yang membagikan hujan di bumi. Namanya terkait dengan gunung dekat danau Chalco dan Xochimilco.",
      "en": "Tomiyauhtecuhtli, \"lord of our ears of grain\", is the Mexica god of grasses and sedges, one of the four Tlaloque who help Tlaloc distribute rain on earth. His name is linked to a mountain near lakes Chalco and Xochimilco.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c01",
        "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c04",
        "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Acaxochitlán ia dihormati sebagai tlaloque arah selatan dan pemilik jagung hitam, yang kini dipadukan dengan Kristus \"Manuelito\"; pemujaan empat penjuru Tlalocan di sana masih hidup.",
      "en": "In Acaxochitlán he is honoured as the tlaloque of the south and owner of black maize, now merged with the Christ \"Manuelito\"; the cult of the four directions of Tlalocan there is still alive.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c02",
        "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c03"
      ]
    }
  ],

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c01",
      "source_id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-s1",
      "quote": "Tomiyauhtecuhtli (del náhuatl: tomiyauhtecuhtli ‘el señor de nuestras espigas’‘to, nuestro; miyauh, espigas; cuatro veces; tecuhtli, señor’) en la mitología mexica es el dios de las gramíneas y de las ciperáceas, identificado como uno de los cuatro Tlaloque.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tomiyauhtecuhtli (bahasa Nahuatl: \"tuan bulir-bulir kami\") dalam mitologi Mexica adalah dewa rumput-rumputan dan teki-tekian, salah satu dari empat Tlaloque.",
        "en": "Tomiyauhtecuhtli (Nahuatl, \"lord of our ears of grain\") in Mexica mythology is the god of grasses and sedges, one of the four Tlaloque."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c02",
      "source_id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-s1",
      "quote": "Al sur, Tomiyauhtecuhtli (señor de la flor del maíz o señor de nuestras espigas de maíz), en la representación del tlaloque dueño del maíz negro, revalorado en el Cristo “Manuelito”, “Señor de Tlamimilolpa”.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Acaxochitlán, Tomiyauhtecuhtli adalah tlaloque arah selatan, pemilik jagung hitam, yang kini dihormati sebagai Kristus \"Manuelito\", Señor de Tlamimilolpa.",
        "en": "In Acaxochitlán, Tomiyauhtecuhtli is the tlaloque of the south, owner of black maize, now revered as the Christ \"Manuelito\", Señor de Tlamimilolpa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c03",
      "source_id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-s1",
      "quote": "En la actualidad, el culto al “Señor de los cuatro rumbos del Tlalocan”, se mantiene vigente, su festividad congrega a los nueve barrios que conforman su actual geografía política",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kini pemujaan \"Tuan dari empat penjuru Tlalocan\" masih hidup, dan perayaannya mengumpulkan sembilan kampung di wilayah itu.",
        "en": "Today the cult of the \"Lord of the four directions of Tlalocan\" is still alive, its festival gathering the nine barrios of the area."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c04",
      "source_id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-s2",
      "quote": "в ацтекській міфології, помічник Тлалока; один із чотирьох тлалоке, відповідальних за розподіл дощу на землі.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Aztek ia pembantu Tlaloc, salah satu dari empat tlaloque yang bertugas membagikan hujan di bumi.",
        "en": "In Aztec mythology he is a helper of Tlaloc, one of the four tlaloque responsible for distributing rain on earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-c05",
      "source_id": "tomiyauhtecuhtli-s2",
      "quote": "Його ім'я пов'язане із однойменною горою, поблизу озер Халко та Сохімілько.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya terkait dengan gunung bernama sama di dekat danau Chalco dan Xochimilco.",
        "en": "His name is linked to the mountain of the same name near lakes Chalco and Xochimilco."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Scultone adalah naga mirip reptil dalam legenda Sardinia dan Korsika yang tatapannya mematikan, dikalahkan Rasul Petrus dengan cermin.",
    "en": "The Scultone is a reptile-like dragon of Sardinian and Corsican legend whose gaze kills, defeated by the apostle Peter with a mirror.",
    "claim_ids": [

      "u-scultone-c02",

    {
      "id": "Scultone adalah hewan mirip reptil seperti naga dalam legenda Sardinia yang membunuh manusia dan hewan; di dekat Baunei, kaburnya naga ini konon membuka jurang Golgo. Versi Korsika menyebutnya hidup di semak-semak dan abadi. Karena tatapannya mematikan, Rasul Petrus memandangnya lewat cermin kecil dan membuatnya tenggelam ke perut bumi.",
      "en": "The Scultone is a reptile-like, dragon-like animal of Sardinian legend that killed people and animals; near Baunei its flight is said to have opened the Golgo chasm. The Corsican version says it lived among the shrubs and was immortal. Because its gaze could kill, the apostle Peter looked at it through a small mirror and sank it into the earth.",

      "id": "u-scultone-c01",
      "source_id": "u-scultone-s1",
      "quote": "Le leggende folcloristiche sarde riferiscono dell'esistenza dello Scultone, un animale rettiliforme simile ad un drago che uccideva uomini e animali: presso Baunei (NU), la fuga di un drago chiamato Scultone avrebbe aperto la voragine del Golgo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda rakyat Sardinia menyebut Scultone, hewan mirip reptil seperti naga yang membunuh manusia dan hewan; di dekat Baunei, kaburnya naga ini konon membuka jurang Golgo.",
        "en": "Sardinian folk legends tell of the Scultone, a reptile-like, dragon-like animal that killed people and animals; near Baunei the dragon's flight is said to have opened the Golgo chasm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "u-scultone-c02",
      "source_id": "u-scultone-s1",
      "quote": "poiché lo sguardo dello  Scultone aveva il potere di uccidere, Pietro lo avrebbe guardato per mezzo di un piccolo specchio, neutralizzando tale potere e facendo sprofondare il Drago nelle viscere della Terra.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena tatapan Scultone dapat membunuh, Rasul Petrus memandangnya lewat cermin kecil, sehingga kekuatan itu hilang dan naga itu tenggelam ke perut bumi.",
        "en": "Because the Scultone's gaze could kill, the apostle Peter looked at it through a small mirror, neutralising that power and sinking the dragon into the bowels of the earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "u-scultone-c03",
      "source_id": "u-scultone-s1",
      "quote": "Lo Scultone compare sotto forma di Basilisco all'interno dei fumetti di Martin Mystère, come principale antagonista della storia \"Il Mistero del Nuraghe\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Scultone muncul dalam wujud basilisk sebagai lawan utama di komik Martin Mystère kisah \"Il Mistero del Nuraghe\".",
        "en": "The Scultone appears as a basilisk, the main antagonist of the Martin Mystère comic story \"Il Mistero del Nuraghe\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "u-scultone-c04",
      "source_id": "u-scultone-s2",
      "quote": "U Scultone ghjè un animale rettiliforme simile à un dracone chì uccidìa omi è animali, vivìa trà l'arbusti è era immortale.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi Korsika, U Scultone adalah hewan mirip reptil seperti naga yang membunuh manusia dan hewan, hidup di semak-semak, dan abadi.",
        "en": "In the Corsican version U Scultone is a reptile-like, dragon-like animal that killed people and animals, lived among the shrubs and was immortal."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Monster Valais adalah binatang buas misterius yang menyerang ternak di Valais, Swiss, pada 1946–1947, yang ternyata kemungkinan seekor serigala.",
    "en": "The Valais Monster was a mysterious beast that attacked herds in Valais, Switzerland, in 1946–1947, most likely a wolf.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "valais-monster-c01",
      "valais-monster-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Monster Valais, disebut juga Serigala Eischoll, adalah julukan bagi binatang buas yang menyerang banyak ternak di kanton Valais, Swiss, sejak April 1946. Pers Swiss menyebutnya monster, dan banyak saksi mengira lynx atau macan kumbang. Namun ia tampaknya seekor serigala Eropa besar yang ditembak di Eischoll pada 27 November 1947.",
      "en": "The Valais Monster, also called the Eischoll Wolf, is the nickname of a beast that attacked many herds in the canton of Valais, Switzerland, from April 1946. The Swiss press called it a monster, and many witnesses thought it a lynx or panther. Yet it seems to have been a large European wolf shot at Eischoll on 27 November 1947.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "valais-monster-c01",
        "valais-monster-c02",
        "valais-monster-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kasus ini dipelajari sebagai desas-desus modern yang menunjukkan kecenderungan orang membayangkan kucing besar berbahaya karena takut; Alexandre Scheurer menyebut persepsi penduduk Valais terhadap satwa \"dikuasai rasa takut dan khayalan\". S. Corinna Bille menulis buku Le Mystère du Monstre pada 1966.",
      "en": "The case is studied as a modern rumour showing people's tendency to imagine dangerous big cats out of fear; Alexandre Scheurer calls the Valais people's view of wildlife \"dominated by fear and fantasy\". S. Corinna Bille wrote Le Mystère du Monstre in 1966.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "valais-monster-c03",
        "valais-monster-c04",
        "valais-monster-c05"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "valais-monster-c01",
      "source_id": "valais-monster-s1",
      "quote": "Valais Monster (French: Monstre du Valais), also known as Eischoll Wolf, and Mysterious animal of Leuk (French: Animal mystérieux de la Loèche), is the nickname of one or more ferocious beasts that attacked numerous herds in the canton of Valais, Switzerland, from April 1946.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Monster Valais (Monstre du Valais), disebut juga Serigala Eischoll atau Hewan Misterius Leuk, adalah julukan bagi satu atau beberapa binatang buas yang menyerang banyak ternak di kanton Valais, Swiss, sejak April 1946.",
        "en": "The Valais Monster (Monstre du Valais), also called the Eischoll Wolf or the Mysterious Animal of Leuk, is the nickname of one or more ferocious beasts that attacked many herds in the canton of Valais, Switzerland, from April 1946."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valais-monster-c02",
      "source_id": "valais-monster-s1",
      "quote": "Among a multitude of supposed identities, those of a lynx or one or several panthers dominate, supported by testimonies followed by many in the media. However, the Valais Monster seems to have been a large European wolf, shot in Eischoll on 27 November 1947.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di antara banyak dugaan, yang paling menonjol adalah lynx atau satu atau beberapa macan kumbang, tetapi monster itu tampaknya seekor serigala Eropa besar yang ditembak di Eischoll pada 27 November 1947.",
        "en": "Among many supposed identities, a lynx or one or more panthers dominated, but the monster seems to have been a large European wolf shot at Eischoll on 27 November 1947."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valais-monster-c03",
      "source_id": "valais-monster-s1",
      "quote": "It testifies to the tendency of the population to imagine the presence of dangerous felines, based on fear and erroneous testimonies.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kasus ini menunjukkan kecenderungan penduduk membayangkan kehadiran kucing besar berbahaya berdasarkan rasa takut dan kesaksian keliru.",
        "en": "The case shows the population's tendency to imagine dangerous big cats, based on fear and mistaken testimony."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valais-monster-c04",
      "source_id": "valais-monster-s1",
      "quote": "According to Alexandre Scheurer, the inhabitants of Valais have a \"perception of fauna dominated by fear and fantasy\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Alexandre Scheurer, penduduk Valais memandang satwa liar dengan \"persepsi yang dikuasai rasa takut dan khayalan\".",
        "en": "According to Alexandre Scheurer, the people of Valais have a \"perception of fauna dominated by fear and fantasy\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valais-monster-c05",
      "source_id": "valais-monster-s1",
      "quote": "In 1966, S. Corinna Bille published a book entitled Le Mystère du Monstre (The Mystery of the Monster), which referred to the whole affair.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1966 S. Corinna Bille menerbitkan buku Le Mystère du Monstre tentang seluruh peristiwa ini.",
        "en": "In 1966 S. Corinna Bille published a book, Le Mystère du Monstre, about the whole affair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valais-monster-c06",
      "source_id": "valais-monster-s2",
      "quote": "La presse suisse utilise le nom de « monstre ».",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pers Swiss memakai sebutan \"monster\" untuknya.",
        "en": "The Swiss press called it a \"monster\"."
      }
    }

      "id": "Dalam mitologi Yunani, Aithe adalah kuda betina Agamemnon, berpasangan dengan Podargos. Echepolos, raja Sikyon, menghadiahkannya kepada Agamemnon sebagai ganti karena tidak ikut perang Troya. Kuda berwarna pirang kemerahan ini bersurai indah dan cepat berlari. Dalam balapan kereta pada pertandingan pemakaman Patroklos, yang dikemudikan Menelaos, Aithe nyaris menyusul Antilokhos.",
      "en": "In Greek mythology Aethe is Agamemnon's mare, paired with Podargos. Echepolus, king of Sicyon, gave her to Agamemnon as compensation for not joining the war at Troy.     {
      "id": "wd-q3607226-c01",
      "source_id": "wd-q3607226-s1",
      "quote": "Nella mitologia greca, Aite era il nome di uno dei cavalli di Agamennone; l'altro si chiamava Podargo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Yunani, Aite (Aithe) adalah nama salah satu kuda Agamemnon; yang lain bernama Podargos.",
        "en": "In Greek mythology Aite (Aethe) was one of Agamemnon's horses; the other was called Podargos."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q3607226-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q3607226-s1",
      "quote": "Echepolo aveva donato i due cavalli ad Agamennone, grande re della Grecia, come risarcimento per non averlo potuto seguire nella sua spedizione bellica a Troia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Echepolos menghadiahkan kedua kuda itu kepada Agamemnon sebagai ganti karena tidak ikut dalam ekspedisi perang ke Troya.",
        "en": "Echepolus gave the two horses to Agamemnon as compensation for not joining his war expedition to Troy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q3607226-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q3607226-s1",
      "quote": "Aite era il cavallo femmina della coppia, aveva una bella criniera ed era veloce nella corsa.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aite adalah kuda betina dari pasangan itu, bersurai indah dan cepat berlari.",
        "en": "Aite was the mare of the pair, with a beautiful mane and swift in the race."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q3607226-c04",
      "source_id": "wd-q3607226-s1",
      "quote": "I cavalli parteciparono ai giochi funebri tenuti per onorare la morte di Patroclo, grande amico dell'invincibile Achille. Durante la corsa, guidata dal fratello del proprietario, Menelao, Aite per poco non riuscì a superare Antiloco.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuda-kuda itu ikut dalam pertandingan pemakaman untuk menghormati Patroklos, sahabat Akhilleus; dalam balapan yang dikemudikan Menelaos, Aite nyaris menyusul Antilokhos.",
        "en": "The horses ran in the funeral games for Patroclus, friend of Achilles; in the race, driven by Menelaus, Aite nearly overtook Antilochus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q3607226-c05",
      "source_id": "wd-q3607226-s2",
      "quote": "Στην ελληνική μυθολογία Αίθη ήταν το όνομα της κοκκινόξανθης φοράδας που χάρισε ο βασιλέας της Σικυώνας Εχέπωλος στον Αγαμέμνονα",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Yunani, Aithe adalah kuda betina berwarna pirang kemerahan yang dihadiahkan raja Sikyon, Echepolos, kepada Agamemnon.",
        "en": "According to the Greek article, Aithe was the reddish-blonde mare that King Echepolus of Sicyon gave to Agamemnon."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Wollunqua adalah dewa ular hujan dan kesuburan suku Warramunga di Australia, variasi Ular Pelangi yang panjangnya bermil-mil.",
    "en": "Wollunqua is the snake god of rain and fertility of the Warramunga of Australia, a miles-long variant of the Rainbow Serpent.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wollunqua-c01",
      "wollunqua-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Wollunqua adalah dewa ular hujan dan kesuburan suku Warramunga (Warumungu) di Northern Territory, Australia, variasi Ular Pelangi yang dikenal banyak suku Aborigin. Ia muncul dari lubang air Kadjinara di Pegunungan Murchison, panjangnya bermil-mil, dan dapat menaruh pelangi di langit. Di depan umum ia disebut urkulu nappaurinnia agar tidak lepas kendali dan melahap orang.",
      "en": "Wollunqua is the snake god of rain and fertility of the Warramunga (Warumungu) of Australia's Northern Territory, a variant of the Rainbow Serpent known to many Aboriginal peoples. He emerged from the Kadjinara waterhole in the Murchison Ranges, is many miles long and can place the rainbow in the sky. In public he is called urkulu nappaurinnia so that he will not break loose and devour people.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wollunqua-c01",
        "wollunqua-c02",
        "wollunqua-c03",
        "wollunqua-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia leluhur totem paruh Uluuru suku Warramunga. Dalam perjalanannya ia bergerak di bawah tanah, muncul untuk melakukan upacara, dan meninggalkan anak-anak roh yang menjadi pusat totem; ia begitu panjang sehingga ekornya tetap di Kadjinara meski kepalanya sudah jauh.",
      "en": "He is the totemic ancestor of the Warramunga's Uluuru moiety. On his journey he travelled underground, surfaced to perform ceremonies and left spirit children that became totemic centres; he was so long that his tail stayed at Kadjinara while his head was far away.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wollunqua-c04",
        "wollunqua-c05",
        "wollunqua-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wollunqua-c01",
      "source_id": "wollunqua-s1",
      "quote": "Wollunqua, also written Wollunka or Wollunkua, is a snake-god of rain and fertility in Australian Aboriginal mythology of the Warramunga people of the Northern Territory of Australia, a variation of the \"Rainbow Serpent\" present in the mythology of many other Aboriginal Australian peoples.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wollunqua, juga ditulis Wollunka atau Wollunkua, adalah dewa ular hujan dan kesuburan dalam mitologi Aborigin suku Warramunga di Northern Territory, Australia, sebuah variasi \"Ular Pelangi\" yang dikenal banyak suku Aborigin lain.",
        "en": "Wollunqua, also written Wollunka or Wollunkua, is a snake god of rain and fertility in the Aboriginal mythology of the Warramunga people of Australia's Northern Territory, a variant of the \"Rainbow Serpent\" known to many other Aboriginal peoples."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wollunqua-c02",
      "source_id": "wollunqua-s1",
      "quote": "The snake, which emerged from a watering hole called Kadjinara in the Murchison Ranges, is said to be many miles long.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular itu muncul dari lubang air bernama Kadjinara di Pegunungan Murchison dan konon panjangnya bermil-mil.",
        "en": "The snake, which emerged from a waterhole called Kadjinara in the Murchison Ranges, is said to be many miles long."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wollunqua-c03",
      "source_id": "wollunqua-s1",
      "quote": "When speaking of the Wollunqua snake in public, the name urkulu nappaurinnia is used, because if they were to call it too often by its real name they would lose control and it would come out and devour them all. It can place the rainbow in the sky at will.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di depan umum ular ini disebut urkulu nappaurinnia, karena bila nama aslinya terlalu sering disebut ia akan lepas kendali, keluar, dan melahap semua orang; ia dapat menaruh pelangi di langit sesuka hati.",
        "en": "In public the snake is called urkulu nappaurinnia, because calling its real name too often would make it break loose, come out and devour everyone; it can place the rainbow in the sky at will."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wollunqua-c04",
      "source_id": "wollunqua-s1",
      "quote": "Wollunqua is a totemic ancestor of the Warramunga people, specifically the Uluuru moiety of the tribe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wollunqua adalah leluhur totem suku Warramunga, khususnya paruh Uluuru suku itu.",
        "en": "Wollunqua is a totemic ancestor of the Warramunga, specifically the tribe's Uluuru moiety."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wollunqua-c05",
      "source_id": "wollunqua-s1",
      "quote": "Setting out towards the east, he travelled at first underground, coming up at various spots where he performed ceremonies and left behind large numbers of spirit children, forming local totemic centres when he passed on.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam perjalanannya ke timur ia mula-mula bergerak di bawah tanah, muncul di berbagai tempat untuk melakukan upacara dan meninggalkan banyak anak roh yang menjadi pusat totem setempat.",
        "en": "Heading east, he first travelled underground, surfacing at various spots to perform ceremonies and leaving many spirit children that became local totemic centres."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wollunqua-c06",
      "source_id": "wollunqua-s1",
      "quote": "for he was so long that although he had travelled very many miles away from his home at Kadjinara, his tail end still remained there.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia begitu panjang sehingga meski kepalanya sudah bermil-mil dari Kadjinara, ekornya masih berada di sana.",
        "en": "He was so long that though his head had travelled many miles from Kadjinara, his tail was still there."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wollunqua-c07",
      "source_id": "wollunqua-s2",
      "quote": "Dans la mythologie aborigène, Wollunqua (encore écrit Wollunka ou Wollunkua) est un dieu-serpent de la pluie et de la fertilité du peuple Warumungu dans le Territoire du Nord, en Australie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Aborigin, Wollunqua adalah dewa ular hujan dan kesuburan suku Warumungu di Northern Territory, Australia.",
        "en": "In Aboriginal mythology Wollunqua is a snake god of rain and fertility of the Warumungu people in Australia's Northern Territory."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "id": "Yauhqueme adalah dewa Mexica tumbuhan, salah satu dari empat Tlaloque pembantu Tlaloc yang membagikan hujan.",
    "en": "Yauhqueme is a Mexica plant god, one of the four Tlaloque who help Tlaloc distribute the rain.",

    {
      "id": "Yauhqueme, \"berpakaian pericón\", adalah dewa Mexica berbagai tumbuhan dan salah satu dari empat Tlaloque pembantu Tlaloc yang membagikan hujan. Namanya terkait dengan bukit dekat Atlacuihuayan, tempat anak-anak dikorbankan bagi dewa hujan, dan ia juga dewa orang Otomi. Ia bertopi kertas, berjuntai bulu, dan memegang kerincingan serta perisai.",
      "en": "Yauhqueme, \"dressed in pericón\", is a Mexica god of plants and one of the four Tlaloque who help Tlaloc distribute rain. His name is linked to a hill near Atlacuihuayan where children were sacrificed to the rain gods, and he was also an Otomi deity. He wore a paper hat and feather train and held a rattle and shield.",

      "en": "In Acaxochitlán he is honoured as the tlaloque of the west, owner of yellow maize, now merged with the Christ of Huayotenco.",
